data-score=\"31\"></p><button type=\"button\" data-reset=\"31\">清空本章作答，重新自测</button><div class=\"question\" id=\"q-32-1\" data-question=\"31,0\"><p class=\"meta\">1. Motive / evidence integrity</p><p><strong>Why does Mara resist the temptation to take the invoices away herself?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. Unauthorized removal could shift the dispute toward her conduct and weaken the usefulness of the records.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. She has concluded that the records contain no information relevant to the hospital.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. She has promised never to challenge restrictions imposed by the supplier.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. She believes that Lane will voluntarily disclose every additional file.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"30\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"32\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v03c09","title":"9. A Duke for One Copper","volume":"3","anchor":"chapter-0033","paragraphs":["Cassian was standing in the kitchen with flour on his cheek when Mara discovered him. His sleeves were rolled up, his apron said ASK THE COOK, and a small girl was instructing him to behave like a donkey. \"A difficult assignment,\" Mara said. \"Your usual expression is so solemn.\" He lifted a bowl. \"I am told that if I spill this, I lose my title.\" \"Finally, a sensible constitutional arrangement.\"","Mercy House's first night market would open at dusk. The theatre curtain had been repaired, the courtyard lights worked, and the refrigerator maintained a reasonably invariable temperature. Tomorrow the clinic would resume a limited schedule. Tonight the residents intended to celebrate by selling food, repaired objects, and services people had volunteered to provide. Agnes was chairperson; Gideon had appointed himself minister of complaints. His first statement concerned the size of breakfast.","The old administrator had called the event an imminent failure in a magazine interview. Mara pinned the article beside the menu. Beneath its alarmist subtitle she wrote: COME AND CHECK. June said the response might heighten the newspaper's interest. \"Excellent,\" Mara replied. \"They can buy soup while they contemplate our decline.\" In keeping with this policy, she reserved the back row for journalists and gave each a refreshment token.","Their most sought-after attraction was to be an auction of useful labor. A carpenter offered to repair a table, a merchant offered delivery, and Ivo offered to remove one troublesome noise from any household machine. Rowan volunteered financial advice. Gideon called it a predatory attempt to make the other prizes look affordable. Cassian offered an evening's work. \"Anything legal,\" he added, catching Mara's eye. \"You remove all joy from negotiation,\" she said.","At hand were beef stew, onion tart, bread with butter, and a cream dessert that Noor had firmly separated from several patients' meal plans. A visiting chemist had brought a vitamin display; Agnes sent him to help with the washing until he stopped trying to sell anything. A physician supplied medical information at a quiet table. The neurologist had come to hear Gideon's piano. For once, the hospital's guests had reasons to be there beyond an examination.","Gideon had regained enough control of his right hand to attempt a short piece. His therapist sat beside him, ready to turn the page. The muscle still tired, and he did not promise a concert. \"I used to dine in a castle after playing this,\" he told Mara. \"Now I dine under a plaster angel holding a pistol. My career has acquired variation.\" She adjusted the light so it did not blur the notes. \"Your audience has improved.\"","In the courtyard, an elderly farmer set up a stall for his daughter's fishery. His twin grandchildren guarded a gold-painted pot containing no gold whatsoever. They declared it a lake monster's birthplace. Cassian, passing with bowls, asked how the monster had crossed the equator. The children demanded he pay for the story. He surrendered a copper coin and was immediately offered a monopoly on future monster purchases. Rowan looked impressed. \"A global corporation in its earliest form.\"","A wealthy collector arrived wearing a smile that made Mara check the location of every small object. He brought a parcel which his attendants placed on the stage. Inside was a polished quartz tower. The collector claimed its latent power could revive memory by restoring the body's \"optimum chromosome rhythm.\" Noor's expression became very still. A pharma company's name decorated the accompanying card, though no actual research was cited.","The collector intended to present the gift in front of the cameras. It would overshadow the modest opening, and in exchange he wanted naming rights over the ward. He alluded to Mara's dependence on generous people. \"One should care about the sentiment,\" he said, when she asked for the evidence behind his claim. \"One should also care about the people who might believe it,\" Mara answered. \"May I examine your marvel before we celebrate?\"","He handed it over. She turned the base, traced a seam, and found the modern glue beneath the supposedly ancient finish. The collector's conceited smile began to deteriorate. Mara did not need an expert in neurology to identify a cheap lamp. She needed the same trained eye that had won at the academy. With a small screwdriver borrowed from Ivo, she opened the cover and revealed a familiar household timer.","\"It can produce light,\" she said. \"It cannot demonstrate the medical result you advertised. A useful distinction, considering that light is already available in this room.\"","Gideon leaned toward the microphone. \"Can it improve the soup?\"","\"No.\"","\"Then my donation remains the more powerful.\"","The crowd laughed. The collector reached for his tower; Mara held it out of reach for one last photograph of the label. He could take back his gift, but he could not take back the claim the cameras had recorded. His attendants carried the enormous box toward the door with considerably less formal ceremony. June later called his departure \"a radical reduction in publicity.\" For now, she merely asked whether he would like to make a correction.","The moment loosened the room. A woman who had quietly paid the clinic's first inspection fee bought three repaired chairs. A local inn ordered weekly bread from the hospital kitchen. Another donor offered to augment the meal fund if the residents published the cost, a condition Agnes accepted. The gains were not a cure for the budget, but they would keep the stove working through the next fortnight. Mara allowed herself to grin. Sometimes people did answer a good proposal with something better than a warning.","Then the auction began. Rowan's financial advice brought six bids and one request for a refund in advance. Ivo sold his services to the woman whose kettle sounded like a hostage begging for release. Cassian stepped onto the stage, and the room became unusually attentive. The donor in the front asked whether he could clean silver. \"Poorly,\" he said. Another asked whether he could dance. \"With a sufficiently patient partner.\"","Mara had one copper coin in her pocket. She raised it as a joke. Pip immediately announced that the duke was reserved for kitchen duty and nobody ought to interfere. The crowd applauded this interpretation of fairness. Rowan, who had clearly planned the entire trap, closed the auction before Mara could withdraw her bid. \"A split-second loss of commercial judgment,\" he told Cassian. \"How will your family cope?\" \"They have a long history of surviving disappointment.\"","Cassian descended the steps and bowed to Mara. \"What did you intend to do with your purchase?\"","\"Apply it to a shortage of clean cups.\"","\"A drastic start.\"","\"I have no intention of allowing a high performer to stagnate.\"","He took the tray from her hands. The look he gave her made the whole noisy room seem briefly secondary. She recovered by handing him a towel. His grin promised that he had noticed her difficulty. She was beginning to suspect he enjoyed being inconvenient almost as much as she did.","Mara went to fetch more cups and saw a shadow at the ward door. The visitor wore an attendant's jacket but had no tray, no patient badge, and no reason to be searching the records cupboard. When he saw her, he took a thin folder and ran. Mara set the cups down. \"Cassian,\" she called, already moving. The man darted through the stage entrance. She knew where the passage ended. He did not.","She caught the swinging door and took the narrow stair to the lighting gallery. Below, the thief raced past a rehearsal donkey and straight into the storage room. Mara crossed the gallery, dropped onto the stage platform, and reached the other exit first. A hanging nylon curtain brushed her face. She pulled it around its pole and stretched it across his route. He hit the cloth, twisted, and crashed into a stack of straw scenery.","Cassian arrived at the opposite door. The thief looked from him to Mara and made the sensible decision to stop. A trace of blood marked his hand where a broken prop had scratched it. Noor would check the injury; a guard would keep him away from the folder. Mara collected the papers from the floor. Her hands shook after the sprint, but the pages were there. She had not lost a single name.","The man refused to explain who had sent him. His pocket contained a reservation card for a warehouse office and a second parcel label. That was enough to pursue in the morning. The folder held the nineteen transfer forms, each with the black square and the strange field for oxygen capacity. Someone still cared enough about those records to steal them. Mara put them in Cassian's evidence case, then went back to the kitchen before the stew could burn.","Gideon began to play. His notes were slower than the recording he remembered, but the melody held. His sister stood in the back row with a hand over her mouth. Nobody pretended that one performance had repaired his memory. Everyone could hear that he was playing now. Mara leaned against the door and let that fact be sufficient for one song.","Cassian found her there with two bowls. \"Your copper coin covered a dance as well,\" he said. She glanced at his apron. \"The uniform may damage your reputation.\" He untied it and dropped it over a chair. \"Then we ought to spare no effort.\" He drew her onto the cleared strip beside the stage. She knew how to follow music. He knew exactly when to stop making jokes. By the time the song ended, she had forgotten the waiting reporters, the collector, and the flour still on his cheek. Evidence / scope</p><p><strong>What does Mara&#x27;s examination of the collector&#x27;s tower establish?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. All gifts offered in return for naming rights must be rejected.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. Every product made by the company named on the card is medically ineffective.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. The collector deliberately caused the memory problems of the hospital patients.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. It is an ordinary lighting object whose visible construction does not substantiate the advertised medical claim.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"31\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"33\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v03c10","title":"10. The Duke in the Fitting Room","volume":"3","anchor":"chapter-0034","paragraphs":["The tailor took one look at Cassian's glue-stained cuff and asked whether the duke had fallen into a carpenter's apprenticeship. \"Willingly,\" Mara said. \"He is showing promise.\" Cassian stood on the fitting platform while Rowan examined a jacket. \"His application to the fashion industry has suffered a setback,\" Rowan observed. \"Fortunately, the industry can still employ me.\"","They had come to a costume repository above the theatre supplier's former office. The business once dressed both actors and society guests, a combination that Mara found highly credible. Its proprietor, Mr Fenn, wanted the repaired costumes valued before a sale. Cassian wanted to inspect the delivery receipts. Rowan had an appointment with the building's owner, who owed his family an explanation and seemed determined to provide a drink instead.","Fenn moved a tape across Cassian's shoulder. \"A difficult body type. Too much muscle for the standard cut.\" \"I shall try to shrink,\" Cassian said. Mara sat on the arm of a chair and watched him undergo the first examination he could not direct. \"You could stop threatening people with that expression.\" \"This is my helpful expression.\" Fenn asked him to turn. \"It needs rework.\"","While the tailor pinned the sleeve, Mara inspected a stage coat said to have belonged to an explorer from Japan. Its maker's label came from Chicago. That did not make the coat a forgery, but the story would need correcting before a purchaser paid for it. In another case lay a mass-produced crown advertised as antique. Rowan lifted it, set it on his head, and declared himself a prince of reduced expectations.","The shop's owner, Mr Dacre, arrived in time to see his cousin wearing the crown. His disapproval was magnificent. He greeted Mara as though he had found an ant in a glass of expensive liquor. \"I hope you understand that these are serious commercial records.\" \"Yes,\" she said. \"I brought a notebook. Should I have brought a pedigree?\"","Cassian's mouth twitched. Dacre decided to address Rowan instead. A business could not survive without protecting its private affairs, he said. The hospital's complaints were eroding goodwill. Rowan asked whether the hospital's payments had been equally private. \"Those were ordinary transactions.\" \"Excellent. Ordinary records will be sufficient.\"","The first file showed fees for storage, delivery, and special handling. One large payment was described as a full-time driver's salary. The driver named in it had worked only nineteen shifts. Mara asked Fenn about the difference. The tailor had seen two drivers, not one. One wore a company uniform; the other arrived in a plain vehicle and always collected a sealed case before the office opened.","Dacre said the second man belonged to the catering division. \"Then the food should have had a considerably better journey than it did,\" Mara replied. He called her interpretation simplistic. She opened the file to a receipt for medical equipment bearing the same dispatch code. The catering explanation no longer covered the document in front of them. Even Dacre could see that.","He tried a different approach. The hospital was a tough business, he said. Its patients were difficult to serve, its kitchen uncompetitive, its future income uncertain. A powerful investor had offered to take the risks. Mara asked whether the investor had told the residents what they would be trading for that protection. \"One cannot explain every technical detail to average people.\" \"But one can ask them to pay every fee. An incredible economy of effort.\"","At the word fee, Fenn looked toward a shoe displayed on a cabinet. Mara noticed. The tailor noticed her noticing and suddenly became fascinated with Cassian's sleeve. She let the conversation move on. Dacre offered to simplify the records into a summary. Rowan said they would prefer the originals. He could be charming when he wanted a favor; he was wonderfully tedious when he wanted an answer.","A crash came from the back room. Fenn's apprentice had knocked over a decorative cannon. Everyone turned except Mara. She slipped off the chair, walked to the cabinet, and picked up the shoe. Its sole had been recently removed and glued back. Something inside shifted against her palm. \"What an interesting heel,\" she said. \"Is this meant to be worn or read?\"","Dacre crossed the room too quickly. Cassian stepped down from the platform with three pins still attached to his jacket. \"Careful,\" he told Dacre. \"We are surrounded by expensive objects.\" Fenn put a small tool into Mara's hand. She eased the loose cover away and found a flat envelope. Inside was the old hospital's signature stamp, wrapped in a receipt for a repair that had never reached Mercy House.","\"A stage prop,\" Dacre said.","Fenn stared at him. \"Then why did you make me sign for it as a confidential return?\"","The room went silent. Mara laid the envelope beside the open file. She had not come to conquer a kingdom. She had come to inspect costumes, and somebody had hidden an instrument of forgery in a theatrical shoe. Sometimes the world did her the courtesy of being as absurd as Rowan claimed it was.","The appointed officer arrived within the hour. Dacre had a great deal to say about misunderstandings. Fenn had the receipt and his own recollection of being told not to discuss the return. His statement would not explain every use of the stamp. It would establish where it had been held and by whom. Mara left the envelope on the desk and accepted a copy of the record. For once, she was happy to take something openly.","On the stairs, Cassian stopped her. \"You looked at the shoe before the noise.\"","\"He looked at it first.\"","\"You did not tell me.\"","\"You were being measured. It seemed unkind to interrupt a man in such distress.\"","He leaned against the wall, laughing without trying to disguise it. The sound warmed her in a way the broad afternoon sunlight had not managed. Fenn appeared in the doorway with a parcel. \"Your jacket,\" he said. \"I have repaired the cuff. Try not to rebuild another hospital in it before Friday.\"","At Mercy House, the first patients had arrived for the reopened clinic. The waiting room no longer smelled of cold coal and damp coats. There were chairs that did not wobble, a water dispenser that worked, and a sign explaining where to ask a question. Agnes kept the loudspeaker silent. The improvement was so ordinary that Mara had to stop in the doorway and look at it twice.","Mila's mother sat near the window with a book. Her heart attack had left her tired, but her complaint that the story's hero was an idiot sounded vigorous. Her doctor had adjusted the care plan; the corrected pharmacy account meant she could obtain the prescribed medicine. Mila had brought a snack instead of leaving her without lunch. Neither woman looked as if her life had become easy. Both looked as if the next step had become possible.","Tomas had an appointment with the neurology specialist. He still could not reconstruct the missing hours. His symptoms could recur, and some questions might remain unanswered for a long time. Yet he wanted to show Mara something else first: a map he had drawn for Pip, with the routes between ports marked in bright colors. The globe's northern hemisphere had acquired a collection of small paper ships. Pip had asked where a submarine's roof went when it rained.","\"I told him it was a matter for a physicist,\" Tomas said. \"He is going to ask Dr Elin Varr.\"","\"We shall all benefit from her opinion,\" Mara replied.","Noor emerged from the clinic and reminded Tomas that he could take his map into the examination if he wanted. His past work was not a test he had to pass, but it could help him explain what had changed. He stood, took the map, and handed Pip the remaining paper ship. The boy held it carefully, as though an important part of a real voyage had been entrusted to him.","In the kitchen, Dr Elin Varr was explaining degrees Celsius to a child who wanted to know whether hot soup could melt the sun. The retired physicist looked entirely in her prime. A nutrient chart had become a drawing board; the only lecture she would give that afternoon concerned the unacceptable size of the committee's teapot. \"My contribution to the academy,\" she told Mara. \"Our standards were in danger of falling off.\"","Rowan offered to buy a larger pot. Agnes asked whether he had considered a job in washing them. The laughter made him seem younger, and for a moment Mara saw how much strain his family quarrel had put on him. She put an extra corn cake on his plate. He looked at it with solemn gratitude. \"An incentive scheme,\" she said. \"The labor market has become extremely competitive.\"","The afternoon post brought a reply from the port clinic. The records request had been accepted in part. Nineteen referral receipts were enclosed, while the medical details remained for the treating professionals and the patients to discuss. Mara spread the permitted copies across a table. Fenn's special-handling dates matched several of them. Some did not. A pattern had begun to emerge without becoming a complete explanation.","Cassian pointed to a line she had missed. Each receipt named the same receiving office at the harbor warehouse. The warehouse also held stock scheduled for removal at the end of the week. They would need access before that happened. Mara reached for her calendar, then found Cassian already holding the pencil. \"Another appointment?\" he asked. \"Possibly.\" \"Shall I wear the jacket?\" She studied the repaired cuff. \"Only if it has room for a notebook.\""],"questions":[{"type":"Evidence / interpretation","stem":"Why does the catering explanation become inadequate during the inspection?","options":["A record bearing the same dispatch code concerns medical equipment rather than catering alone.","Mara dislikes the owner's attitude toward ordinary people.","The tailor admits that all costumes in the repository are forged.","The named driver has worked at the business for fewer years than the owner."],"answer":0,"explanation":"关键是同一运输代号出现在医疗设备收据上，使“只有餐饮业务”的解释不能覆盖眼前文件。人物态度、工作年限与部分服装估值不构成这一具体反驳的依据。","evidence":[8,9],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1617,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0034\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">3</div><h1>10. Evidence / interpretation</p><p><strong>Why does the catering explanation become inadequate during the inspection?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. A record bearing the same dispatch code concerns medical equipment rather than catering alone.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. Mara dislikes the owner&#x27;s attitude toward ordinary people.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. The tailor admits that all costumes in the repository are forged.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. The named driver has worked at the business for fewer years than the owner.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"32\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"34\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v03c11","title":"11. The Woman in the Rafters","volume":"3","anchor":"chapter-0035","paragraphs":["Mara was halfway up the theatre's roof when Cassian called her name. She looked down through the rain. He stood beside Ivo with a safety line in one hand and the expression of a man whose soul had temporarily left to complain elsewhere. \"I am attached to the line,\" she called. \"I noticed.\" \"Then stop looking as though you intend to arrest the roof.\" Ivo coughed into his sleeve.","The trouble had begun with an old manuscript and a new eviction demand. The demand treated the theatre and Mercy House as a single commercial property, free for sale once the clinic's charitable work ceased. A patient, a staff member, and a broken piano had all been described as remaining contents. Mrs Bell read the phrase twice. \"I shall write to them,\" she said. \"My value is considerably greater than the piano's.\"","She remembered a copy of the original foundation deed. The hospital's twins had deposited it above the stage during a flood, alongside the theatre's earliest programs. A later architect had sealed the access. Ivo had checked the old plan against the building; the chest was still visible behind a narrow attic window. The safe route was outside, not through the unstable ceiling. He had prepared a line, checked its anchors, and placed a ladder where Mara could reach it.","She could have left the retrieval to a specialist. One was available next week. The sale application would be considered tomorrow. Mara, who had spent her childhood getting into places adults had declared impossible, did not consider a dusty window a reason to surrender the house. The aerial path took her past the broken statue of a winged horse. Its bronze nose pointed toward the attic. \"Do not inspire me,\" she told it. \"I am already being sufficiently foolish.\"","Below, the kitchen kept working. Illness did not wait for a property dispute, and neither did hunger. The visiting physician had arranged medical care for two patients with new symptoms. Agnes was balancing the nutrition plans against the stock of ham and cucumber. Pip had announced that the courtyard ought to host NBA players once the drain dried. Gideon said he would settle for one adult who could throw a ball without breaking a window.","At the roof's turn, Mara found the latch painted shut. She could discern the outline beneath a crust of old varnish. It needed patient work, not strength. A careless blow could shatter the pane beside her face. She used the restorer's tools she had carried in her bag, loosening the edge a fraction at a time. The window finally gave with a small, indignant scratch. A gust blew her hair into her eyes.","\"Status?\" Cassian called.","\"The house has opinions.\"","\"That was established when it dropped a letter on me.\"","She laughed, and the laugh steadied her. His fear had not disappeared. He was keeping the line secure anyway. She slipped through the window, lowered herself onto a solid beam, and let her eyes adjust. The attic smelled of dry wood and the mineral dust of a long-forgotten repair. A narrow path led to the chest. Everything else was a deceptive sheet of plaster over empty air.","On the chest lay a child's drawing of a springbok, faded almost white. Mara thought of Pip's picture downstairs. Different generations had apparently reached the same conclusion about the proper wildlife for a hospital. A rolled program announced a benefit after a bombing years before the twins established the current foundation. The money had supplied blankets and medicine. The building's history was less imperial grandeur than people repeatedly making room for one another.","She found a second box of hospice records and left it closed. Its existence might matter to the archive; its contents did not belong in her curious hands. The deed was in a marked compartment at the front of the chest. The seal had survived. So had a duplicate attached to the founding rules. Mara photographed their position, placed the permitted documents in a protective sleeve, and prepared to go back the way she had come.","A crack sounded near the window. Someone below had started moving the ladder.","Mara froze. Through the gap, she could see a man in a maintenance coat, his face hidden by the hood. He was pulling the lower section away from the wall. Cassian turned from the line toward him. \"Leave it.\" The man shoved the ladder sideways and ran. Ivo caught its base before it could tumble. The sudden movement shook the frame beside Mara's shoulder, but the safety line held.","Cassian could have pursued the intruder. He stayed with the rope. Mara saw the choice and put the sleeve inside her coat. A foot of the ladder had come off the stone. She could not simply climb down until it was secure again. \"There is a lower window,\" she called. \"Stage side. Three metres to my left.\" Ivo looked at the plan, then at the real wall. \"I can get that one open. Wait.\"","The wait lasted perhaps a minute. Mara's body gave it the shape of an hour. She could hear a van engine beyond the yard and see the white flash of its windscreen. Then the engine stopped. Rowan appeared in the doorway holding the van keys. June had stood in front of the vehicle and begun asking the driver whether he was a civilian contractor or an exceptionally poor escape artist. He had declined to be filmed and remained very still.","Ivo opened the lower window. Mara moved along the beam, lowered herself to the sill, and caught the inside edge. Her boot slipped once. The line tightened before she could fall. Cassian's voice reached her, steady and precise. \"I have you.\" She found the ledge again. The second movement was easier. Ivo caught her hand, she swung inside, and the two of them landed in a tremendous pile of obsolete scenery.","A cardboard dragon's head rolled past the door. Rowan looked in. \"I assume the retrieval is complete. Unless you have been swallowed by local folklore.\" Mara sat up with dust in her hair and a ridiculous amount of relief in her chest. \"My kingdom has suffered a setback.\" \"Your kingdom is serving lunch. They would like their chief troublemaker back.\"","Cassian came in last. He did not begin with a lecture. He knelt, looked at the shallow scrape on her wrist, and helped her stand. She put her forehead against his for one brief second before the others could make a joke. \"The rope held,\" she said. \"Yes.\" His hand was still closed around its end. \"I know.\" She covered that hand with hers until his fingers loosened.","The man in the maintenance coat had been caught at the gate by the real building contractor. His authorization covered removal of a broken aerial, not the ladder. He claimed he had misunderstood. His employer would have questions to answer; the local officers took his statement. Mara wanted to know who had sent him. Cassian wanted that too. Neither of them needed the answer before making sure nobody else could reach the roof equipment.","Noor cleaned Mara's scrape and sent her to wash before coming near the kitchen. \"I can survive a little dust,\" Mara protested. \"The patients need not participate in your experiment.\" The answer ended the discussion. Mara returned with damp hair, an embarrassed expression, and an appetite that astonished Agnes. A bowl of soup, a cucumber sandwich, and something sweet restored her interest in civilization.","The deed did not make her owner of the house. It did establish a distinction the sale application had omitted: the hospital's charitable rooms could not be transferred under the theatre lease alone. An independent review would still be needed, but the residents now had a specific document on which to rely. Their objection would not consist only of pleading that eviction would hurt. Mara took the sleeve to the appointed officer before the afternoon deadline.","By evening, the sale application had been paused. Nobody called it a permanent rescue. Everyone in the ward called it an excellent reason to have cake. Mrs Bell, whose anaemia had limited her performance but not her opinions, demanded a larger slice than Gideon's. He objected that equality had suffered a fatal blow. She explained that she had supplied the clue and he had supplied criticism. The committee declined to arbitrate.","A retired priest arrived with a donation from his congregation. He said his ordination had not prepared him to negotiate cake distribution. His companion, a nurse from the hospice, said nothing in human history could. They joined the table, where a conversation about childhood led to an argument over whether the plumber's singing counted as music. For a little while, the only thing Mercy House had to defend was the last sandwich.","Cassian found Mara at the doorway after the plates were cleared. She was looking at the stone arch, now missing its threatening brass H. \"You came back for the rope,\" she said. \"You could have caught him.\" \"I had something else to hold.\" It was a simple answer. She did not tease him for it. Instead she kissed the flour-free corner of his cheek, and discovered that a man capable of facing a hostile board could still forget how to speak.","June emerged carrying her coat and took one look at him. \"Has she stolen another key?\" \"No,\" Mara said. \"Something considerably less portable.\" Cassian recovered enough to open the door for them both. His hand brushed her back as she passed, warm through the cloth. The question of what she had stolen could wait. The next morning, the warehouse would open its records room, and the house they had saved for now would have people waiting for their return."],"questions":[{"type":"Inference / limitation","stem":"What is the immediate significance of the founding deed?","options":["It proves who ordered the interference with Mara's ladder.","It automatically makes Mara owner of both the theatre and the hospital.","It permanently cancels every debt owed by the residents.","It supplies a specific basis for challenging the proposed transfer of the hospital rooms."],"answer":3,"explanation":"契据区分医院慈善用房与剧院租约，因此住户获得具体抗辩依据；仍需独立复核。文本明确否认女主因此成为房主，也没有把房屋文件说成袭击者身份的证据。","evidence":[22],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1632,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0035\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">3</div><h1>11.
